Accordingly, a day block time of 10 ‘Vienna hours’ has been agreed, so that the following speaking times arise: ÖVP and SPÖ each 175 minutes, Free 120 minutes and Green 130 minutes. The following speech time agreement was also reached for the debates during the period until 13.00, which were transmitted by the ORF: one word notice per group of 15 minutes, then the vice-president with 15 minutes, then one word message per group with 10 minutes each; and another the State Secretary at the Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs. for transport, innovation and technology with 10 minutes, then one speech per group of 10 minutes each, one member of the government with 5 minutes and one word per group each with 5 minutes. The remaining speaking time by 1 p.m. will be distributed by the President-in-Office, after consultation with the presidents of the club, to the four political groups in such a way that all political groups can speak for the same period. There is also agreement that actual corrections will only be called after 1 p.m. That's what the Hoge Haus decides. We shall now proceed to the vote. I ask those ladies and gentlemen who agree to the proposal for a sign. – This is unanimously accepted. We shall therefore proceed in this way. 1. Miedl Taferl placed on the speaker's desk]] [[Congratulation with the Greens and the SPÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen! If one has such a large package of reforms – as it is rightly claimed – then one has, of course, to ask first and foremost a question that the ancient Romans have always asked themselves, namely: Cui bono? – For whom is it good? If you look at this reform work and analyze it according to this question, you get to know relatively quickly who the whole thing is good and can become good for. You said it was good for the taxpayer because he would save money. Take note of this at last! For example, the Swiss railway can obtain cross-subsidisation from the road toll for its infrastructure. This is something that has already been completely excluded in Austria in the light of the EU directives. Please also note that the transport of goods in Switzerland is organized in its own stock company, ladies and gentlemen. Take note, too, that the productivity of the Swiss railway is 30 percent higher than in Austria, because there are relatively much fewer employees there. This means that the corresponding transport services are provided there with fewer employees than in Austria. ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e16256	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! Since the announcement of Federal Chancellor Schüssel in the course of the last National Council election campaign to place the Animal Protection Act on a federal basis, and since the publication of the currently present completely inadequate draft animal welfare law, detailed measures such as the Animal Transport Act have also been taken into the public's eye. Although this novel provides for an adjustment of the animal transport law required by EU directives, it must be noted that the Austrian agricultural lobby, in close cooperation with the Austrian milling lobby, once again managed to make a sensible law a toothless papermaker. The Austrian solution to the necessary administrative burden for the administration of the legal situation already in force is that: It is ensured that the audited themselves will be checked and documented in good form on official forms. It is true that the entrepreneur issues the billing papers for animal transport itself. According to the officials in the competent ministry, the entrepreneurs have of course never objected to themselves, although the competent administration knows from information from control bodies that, for example, 70 percent in Carinthia and about 50 percent in Salzburg are subject to animal transport. In view of these shortcomings in the field of animal transport, all the promotions for this area should therefore be removed, and this is our opinion, because the only losers on the basis of these conditions are the animals that would actually be the only ones to be protected by these laws. We therefore call for the checks of animal transport to be substantially strengthened and carried out by competent veterinary authorities. We therefore also call for animal transport controllers to be employed in all federal states. We also demand that suitable vehicles be used, even in the case of short transport routes. We also call for annual reporting on controls to the EU Commission and the National Council. This novel, ladies and gentlemen, does not, from our point of view, meet the requirements of human animal welfare, and therefore we Social Democrats will not give our consent to this proposal.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e16420	Mr President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! I would love to take my previous speaker by word. He's been talking about animals being fellow creatures. – Mr colleague, I would like to invite you very cordially if I find you somewhere in the hall. Oh, yes, you sit up there – to express yourself in your own party and at the Chancellor’s office that, for example, in connection with the Federal Animal Protection Act – to which there is now a draft, in which there is a lack of substance – one is committed to upholding the demand of the animal welfare people’s desire, namely to lay down animal protection in the Constitution – as a safeguard of the dignity of fellow creatures. That is completely missing in this draft. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the SPÖ.]] What is missing in the Federal Animal Protection Act – clearly, you almost have to say – is the area of animal transport. It is therefore all the more important to pay close attention to this law, which constitutes an EU directive implementation, and to see exactly what changes it brings. First, of course, it is necessary – and more than overdue – to implement EU legislation and directives in Austria. Secondly, I am sorry to say that the existing Austrian animal transport law is clearly outraged, which means that we are being prompted by the EU to adopt regulations that are less sharp, less stringent than the Austrian animal transport law that has so far been applied. I would like to mention just two examples: Under this law to be adopted today, animal transports – at least announced – should last only eight hours and cover a maximum of over 500 kilometres. – That’s not quite true, and I want to clear up with this little myth: The de facto regulation for the decision – and I think you have to think about it well – provides that nine hours must not be exceeded – there are 45 minutes of breaks by the driver – and then a 12-hour break must be held, but the animals do not have to be unloaded, and then a nine-hour transport, with 45 minutes of break, takes place again. This can be repeated at any time. Where there is the advantage for animal welfare and the prevention of animal suffering – compared to the Austrian law, which now provides a maximum of six hours and 130 kilometers or, if it is on the motorway, 260 kilometers – you have to ask yourself. Now, of course, it is clear that the Austrian law cannot regulate EU-wide animal transport, where the biggest fragments of the problem lie. I would also like to address the cause of the problem and not just the control of the symptoms. A large proportion of the cause of the problem lies in the EU support system, which continues to grant export refunds on live exports to third countries, such as Egypt or Lebanon, which means that animal transport is carried out there. In addition, this situation is not so uninteresting in relation to our transit problem, because some of these transports are of course also transit traffic, which is mainly through Tyrol, but not only through Tyrol. In other words, we should take seriously discussions that have been held in this House long before I started to work as a Member of Parliament, in which we had already agreed to take action in the EU to prohibit the transport of live animals and export refunds. – To this day, I do not know that the Federal Government would have made itself very committed to this. A second example to illustrate the difference between the current regulation and the existing Austrian laws: There are known cases such as those where a transport was picked up at the burner years ago, where it was found that the chickens to be transported, which had been peared in cages, were stifled by the falling excrements of the above hens, who were frozen. The Austrian Animal Transport Act contains rules on the fact that it must not at all be necessary for dirt or excrements to fall on animals on lower floors. – We do this with today’s de facto template. [[Abg. Wattaul: That's not true!]] [[Abg. ==References====External links== Wattaul)... prove that this is not correct?]] This is no longer in and out of the EU Directive. It is therefore all the more important, I believe, that we are devoted to what we can at least make quite clear on our own. It would be, on the one hand, to regulate in the law what we can regulate, for example, to define: What do we mean by rest periods? To what extent are they to be observed? What do we understand when we say that animals must have “adequate ” space? What do we mean when we say that “lightly injured” animals may be transported? What is a “light injury ”? – If a leg is broken without this “only” and the animal is standing for hours? – Technically speaking, this is a slight injury, but practically speaking, it is animal torture. Why can we not agree to provide clear definitions in this law? The second, where we can do a lot and where I still do not give up the hope that something will go on, is control. Control is certainly the most efficient instrument available to us. We have also repeatedly given rise to criticism in EU reviews. Here we have opportunities to expand and improve things. A request has already been submitted, where I am very glad that there is a four-party agreement that at least one wants to keep the existing state, so that the animal inspectors, who have so far been supported by the customs guard and who could not carry out their checks without support by the executive, do not simply remove this support on a case-by-case basis, but jointly seek to ensure that this support continues to be granted to the necessary extent and, at least, to the extent so far. However, we will continue to have additional trained animal inspectors at our disposal in the future because, with the accession of our neighbouring countries to the EU, there is a lack of border controls and thus also of animal welfare controls. I think we should use this trained staff to actually control animal transport on the road and at the borders. In the course of the transit discussion, Minister Gorbach announced that he would be more responsible for ensuring that all rules relating to transit are properly complied with. I am really excited and I have to confess that my confidence in the announcements has been seriously shaken this morning and afternoon, but one can hope that the checks will be carried out at least in the field of animal protection as announced and that we will take advantage of instruments that will work: for example, the establishment of laboratories for animal transport, where one can see with the appropriate expertise in the checks whether animals have obviously not been watered for many hours. [[Interim note of Vice-Cancellor Gorbach]] You can see this – when you know yourself; that is, of course, the hooks in it. So we can set up labs, as was the case in Tyrol, for example, where then stopped transports, which obviously violate current animal welfare regulations, are being traced back. And in Tyrol, for example, it has been found that with efficient controls, with lab stations and with genuinely consistent compliance with the control requirements, the animal transports – see and marvel! – could be reduced by half. This must also be a goal for the national efforts so that we actually take animal welfare seriously. Finally, a general comment on animal welfare: I think we must be very, very careful, even in this draft law, so that we do not automatically become dependent on lower EU levels. There are scopes in the EU Directives. Unfortunately, these are not used in any way with the present draft. We would have much more room here to implement things in the interests of animal welfare. In any case, we have a lot of scope in a federal uniform animal welfare law, scope for us not to conform to minimum EU standards, but to the highest standards that our own federal states have long achieved. However, in this respect, the assessment draft, which Federal Chancellor Schüssel has now presented, unfortunately goes a long way behind. I hope – not only in the sense of the popular desire to protect animals, but above all in the sense of the animals that are likely to be affected in the coming years, for which the law will apply – that the assessment draft will make significant improvements.

Secondly, How much experience does the doctor have? How does his learning curve look? How many cases per year does he look after? Thirdly, How much time does he have to go to the patient? Fourth: Is it possible to obtain a second opinion or even a third opinion? This too is part of a quality system and will also be part of the reform efforts of the Minister. As I said, Every mistake can mean a personal disaster, and every mistake is actually a mistake too much. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e18613	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! The Animal Materials Act to be adopted today regulates the disposal and recovery of animal waste. Of course, the fact that proper delivery and safe disposal of animal materials is in public interest is undisputed. The fact that this law is particularly important for infectious reasons and, of course, for the protection of human and animal health is, I think, clear to the opposition parties. The new federal legislation provides for a free choice of disposal plant for slaughterhouse operators. However, there is an obligation to conclude written agreements with authorised waste disposal plants, which will also improve control and traceability. The more detailed provisions on the notification, delivery, transfer and takeover of animal materials intended for disposal may be laid down by the head of state by regulation, and the condition for the creation of local collection systems, such as the biotonne, will then be subject to the head of state. The definition of cost-covering tariffs for the disposal of animal waste has been maintained, taking into account the Community provisions for certain exceptional cases, for example for case-laws, where, in these cases, the head of state may provide for different cost schemes, taking into account the economic performance of the owners of animals. And this is exactly the point, which Mr Maier also presented as very controversial, but I am sure that, in the sensitive area of the disposal of animal waste, the competence should lie with the countries, in the sense of subsidiarity, because it is possible for countries to respond very quickly and efficiently to waste disposal tasks which can be very different due to regional specificities. The fact that the head of state is given the opportunity to provide for a different cost regime for animal farmers in particularly difficult economic situations can be supported by a regulation, as this can be a very important aid in certain emergency situations. I am thinking, for example, of animal owners who, due to disasters which they themselves cannot influence, are of course having great difficulties in dealing with the disposal of the destroyed animals financially. This can be local problems such as floods, lightnings, fires, but it is also in individual cases, so when it comes to removing individual animals It is a very high cost, especially when these animals come from heavy animals, i.e. from cattle or horses, and if they have to be transported from distant holdings or from very exposed locations, such as high alpine areas. Then it can already happen that the animal owner is simply not able to bear these disposal costs in fact. And that is where the head of state can provide for special regulations. For small and micro-enterprises, these regulations, which can be specifically set up by the head of state, are of economic necessity, because this is sometimes really about the further existence of these plants. In this sense, I am convinced that this animal material law is increasingly bringing security for the future to producers, processing plants and, above all, consumers, and that in this sense it is really possible for all parties to give their consent to this animal material law.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e19486	Mrs Frau Bundesminister Rauch-Kallat has now spoken. - Please, Mrs. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e19504	Mr President. High house! The present government template is a result of an Article 15a Treaty, which was concluded between the Federal Government and the Länder to, after decision-making in the Structural Commission, an updated information and report system in hospitals with effectiveness 1. ==References====External links== Following the content of the amendments to the working and expert bodies of the Structural Commission, including representatives of the federal states and representatives of the hospitals and other institutions, the further procedure was agreed at the meetings of the Structural Commission in June and in October. The Government template, which is pending the decision today, contains: the summary and harmonisation of various data reports on a single annual report, as the diagnostic and performance reports, including intensive reports, the health care account and the health care statistics have been transmitted separately and at different dates; Furthermore, a reduction in reporting dates, some of which also provide more time for the reporting, which improves both the completeness and the quality of the reports; Thirdly, the removal of redundant data reports; so far, some data have been collected several times in different reports and reported to the Ministry; fourthly, the introduction of nationally defined automation-aided completeness and plausibility tests, which facilitates and accelerates the testing and improves the comparability of the data of the various hospitals; and, ultimately, a faster availability of valid data and evaluations. Since the above-mentioned automation-assisted test programmes are available to and are to be used by the hospitals and the Landesanstalts, the Ministry is already receiving double-checked data, and examinations by the Ministry are therefore no longer necessary. On the question of Mr Kaipel on the outstanding regulations: There are a total of four regulations which were already under review and which will be adopted in December. Their content is: the re-establishment of the existing regulations, the implementation of the Documentation Act and the adaptation of terms. Thank you.

Package of medicinal products included in the Social Insurance Amendment Act is a comprehensive package of medicinal products, in which structures that have lasted for many years but have not optimally contributed to the rapid and unbureaucratic development of the authorisation of medicinal products, or that the costs could be partially kept in mind. In the course of the negotiations on this drug package, we have included all those concerned – the pharmaceutical industry, the wholesale, the pharmacies, including the house pharmacies, the medical profession – in order to ultimately achieve the best possible result for patients – for the insured, in order to represent their interests. I think that by re-establishing a refund code, it will be ensured that, in the future, drug innovations will be more quickly refundable, that the path of admission will be more unbureaucratic and, above all, more transparent and comprehensible. A cost control and a quantity control – and without that it will not be possible – will be to regulate social security and the doctors, which is unbureaucratic. You can rest assured that I will ensure that not the Holy Bureaucratius will celebrate his joyful originals here, as some officials – rather officials – would say that officials are far more flexible inside. With this novel, or with the regulations I signed this afternoon on the trading margins in wholesale and the trading margins in the pharmacies and house pharmacies, this package has not included a total savings potential of 120 million schilling in the coming year, and this is important – the effect that results from increased use of generics. These figures are around €37 million in the next year and then up to €90 million in 2006, so that the total package from 2004 to 2006 will save about €600 million in total. But we must not be fooled by this: This is not €600 million, which the social security funds spend less than today, but the costs compared to today will rise from €2.2 billion in 2002 to around €2.5 billion by 2006. This is necessary because, on the one hand, demographic development, i.e. the age of our population, is increasing more and more – and thus the need for medical treatment is also given. At the same time, there are always new drugs that naturally have high research costs and are therefore also offered at appropriate high prices. We want the Austrians to have these medicines available to them as quickly as possible and immediately as possible. At the same time, we have also brought reliefs for the insured, i.e. the annoying course, the pilgrimage or that of many people as a shock. the path to the head doctor will be missed in the future for patients. It will be especially for generics to pay a lower prescription fee, which means you get the third for free at two generics. We hope that this will also provide an appropriate attractiveness to use these generics, together with the discussion with the doctor, the doctor's explanation for the use of medications. What's particularly important to me is: We have agreed that this drug package also includes a very large information campaign for insured persons on the use and also abuse of medicines, on the use of drugs. I think that this will contribute substantially to the health of patients, because it shows that an excess of medications does not always lead to more health. Quite the contrary: occasionally the use of the “green recipe” would make sense. In this sense, I believe, we have taken a first, very important step towards health reform with this drug package, knowing that it is only a priority of reform and not yet the entire reform. We will have to work very hard in the next few months, especially in the structural area, at the interfaces between intra- and extra-mural areas, in order to achieve improvements, which will also have to lead to cost savings. We will have to reduce overcapacity, but at the same time avoid under-supply. For example, when I think about the care of psychotherapy, when I think of pediatric medicine, when I think of hospice: We have too many cots and too few cots. Here, it will be useful to convert beds and, above all, to improve the interface between the hospital and the established doctors. I would like to invite you to support us.

We were also told about the loadability of old and young in practice. I think it makes a lot of sense to introduce a certain range of sites, and that is why this amendment to the Federal Act provides that it is possible to establish that up to eight services are permitted by operating agreement or by agreement with staff representatives. ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23723	We're going to the 13th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting in so far as the rapporteur was the first speaker. Mrs Marek, I give the floor to you.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23748	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The protection provisions on maternity protection in Austria are all clearly above the minimum standards laid down by the International Labour Conference. For example, the total term of protection at 16 weeks by 2 weeks is above the required minimum duration. In the case of multiple births and redundancies due to imperial cuts, this period increases by 4 weeks, which also meets the minimum standards required. The same applies to the weekly allowance, the amount of which is the average monthly income, including 13th and 14ths. In terms of salary. [[President Dr. Khol will again take over the presidency.]] For this reason, there is no need for any change in this area for the Republic of Austria, which is once again proof of the high social standards in our country. Our comprehensive Mother Protection Act was adopted in 1957 after a long phase of birth – you can almost say – already at the end of the 19th century. ==References====External links==* Official website This law made Austria the country in the circle of the Western industrialized states with the most extensive maternity protection regulations, and we are still in the international comparison, which does not mean that we could not get any better. The Convention on the subject here contains, among other things, two recommendations which I would like to address briefly: Firstly, the extension of the term of protection to 18 weeks is recommended, which I consider to be particularly superior to those women who are employed in occupations with increased physical stress. I would be very pleased if we could create reliefs for these women. The second recommendation of the International Labour Conference relates to flexibility in the situation or division of the term of protection before and after delivery. I think that we must be very, very careful in such a flexibilisation in the sense of efficient worker protection, so that women do not put too much pressure on the employer. Finally, however, I would like to return to the current Mother Protection Act. Mr Grete Rehor, later Minister for Social Affairs and Social Affairs, was present at the National Council meeting on 13 March. One of the applicants to the Mother Protection Act on March 1957, and I do not wish to abstain from the minutes of this sitting the conclusions of Grete Rehor's speech. I quote: "The Federal Chancellor may claim that in recent years he has played a decisive part in the development of important social policy laws with the greatest understanding and with the use of his personality, in particular then if the competent parties could not agree. We must now also present him to the House as a birth aid for the Mother Protection Act. In the last, decisive round, he has helped us that the law as it stands can be passed on to the decision. Since the Austrian Federal Chancellor is at the same time the Federal Party of the Austrian People's Party, the representatives of the Austrian People's Party and, in particular, the representatives of the Austrian Workers' and Workers' Confederation can be pleased to note that we have played a decisive part in the coming to this law." ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23812	The next speaker is Mrs Mag. Kuntzl. 3 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e23830	Ladies and gentlemen! As my previous speaker has already said, this Convention defines minimum standards which do not meet the autonomous national rules which are above these minimum standards. It is noteworthy, however, that this Convention sets out rules beyond the existing Convention. For example, the protection of termination is extended in time to the entire pregnancy, the term of protection and a period to be determined by law thereafter. In the old scheme, the absolute protection of termination only referred to the period during maternity leave. Furthermore, this Convention explicitly states that a unilateral termination of the employment relationship by the employer may by no means be due to the pregnancy or the term of protection or for reasons related to it. Furthermore, a ban on discrimination on grounds of motherhood is explicitly renewed. As a third point, I would like to stress that there is a more stringent burden of proof for the cancelling employer, who must now prove that the reasons for the termination of the employment relationship are in no way related to pregnancy. To sum up, then, this is an improvement in the defined minimum standards, which we can only welcome, and we will therefore agree to this proposal.

To date, the previous agreement has only been ratified by 27 countries, including Austria. This recast of the Convention on the Mother protection is necessary in order to enable ratification through the flexible design of a larger number of states.

I would ask that more work be done in this direction, especially in Austrian foreign policy, so that many more countries join and sign such agreements. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25548	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! At this first reading, we are dealing with a request from my colleague, Mr Mag. Sima, with which the Waste Management Act should be amended. As we all know, the types of packaging and the materials are becoming more and more diverse and more unclear for consumers. Especially in the case of beverage packaging, the consumer is hardly able to quickly see whether the product is subject to the ban or not. In addition, the proportion of multi-way packaging is dramatically declining especially in drinks. The consumer should be given a quick overview by means of a clear label on the packaging with a large ‘P’ and the word ‘Pfand’, both when buying and when returning. Of course, the freedom of design should not be restricted. Ladies and gentlemen! ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25583	The next speaker is Mr Steindl. 2 minutes speaking time. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25602	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, In view of the late hour, I will make my “bottle topic” as short as possible. The present proposal ensures that environmentally conscious consumers will be able to obtain a better overview of pledged packaging through the clear labelling of the goods concerned with a ‘P’ and the word ‘P’ on the packaging, both in the purchase decision in the business and in terms of the return of the product. I think that this proposal is quite reasonable. However, I can't afford the freedom to design. I strongly support uniform labelling in order to make it easier for consumers. I strongly reject the deposit system according to the German model, in which all disposable packaging must be paid for drinks. This would destroy our well-functioning glass collection system. 70 percent of the collected glass are still beverage packaging. If one had to return these disposable packagings in the store, the collection system would no longer be expected to produce 90,000 containers for the rest of Austria. So ecologically, this would hardly be justified. More than 200 000 of the 230 000 tons of glass in Austria are already being collected and reused. Finally, I would like to ask my esteemed colleague, Mr Scharer, whether Mrs Landeshauptsteller Gabi Burgstaller lives in a rental home of her husband or, as Mr Broukal did in his actual correction, in an apartment. – What’s really true now? Dear ladies and gentlemen! I wish you all a peaceful and happy Christmas.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e25796	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! This request implies that even where Federal Police Councils are set up, the penalties should be paid to the countries, cities and social assistance organisations. It is basically true that there is a plan of commitment for fines and that they should be sent to social assistance. But it is completely incomprehensible to me that there is a replacement scheme introduced by the 2000 budget accompanying law, so that it is everywhere where federal police directors are set up that money goes to the federal government, i.e. to the Ministry of the Interior. This, ladies and gentlemen, is unfair, illogical, rule of law and is unsocial. Let me also say the following: In 2000, this was a purely monetary move by the Minister of the Interior, once again at the expense of the cities, the countries and also the social assistance organisations responsible for people when they get into poverty. Here I support the concerns of many ÖVP mayors and – because Klaus Wittauergerade is here – also of the Innsbruck Deputy Mayor Sprenger of the ÖVP, who has put these demands in the municipal council. This is to be repaired in the sense of justice for the cities and the countries responsible for social assistance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2003-12-04-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00041_d8e26057	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! We are in fact only more concerned with the fact that we want to create a Federal Office for the Transport of Goods, which should consider the street for work. And we are not concerned with creating a new authority and setting up new officials, but we are concerned that, if you already check freight traffic, you should examine it comprehensively by an authority, similar to that in Germany. They have been very efficient testing methods there and have also been able to detect a case such as Kralowetz. With us, it is currently that the whole is divided into around 17 authorities, and it would be useful and targeted to make the workplace street safer to summarize all of this in an authority that then checks on the road, and it would not be even more distributed on ASFINAG et cetera and possibly even unemployed people to act on the street like auxiliary workers. I invite everyone to discuss this matter seriously in committee. We do not want to raise the claim here that we are the only knowledgeable, but we should consider in principle: What can be broken out and what should be checked in an authority? - Thank you very much.
